Through the Wrong Spyhole","volume":"婚后外传：失踪公爵与木头珍珠","anchor":"chapter-0203","paragraphs":["Behind the wall, Cassian was drinking excellent tea while an actor explained the next scene. His supposed kidnap had become a debate about whether a duke could move a sofa. The answer was yes, provided he waited for another person to lift the other end. He had removed his coat, rolled his sleeves and become considerably more distracting than the game designer intended.","Mara watched through the marked spyhole. Her primal urge was to abandon the clues and take him home. The common-sense alternative was to open the actual door. She could redefine a rescue as a visit if necessary. A good host might generously offer her a cup before pretending to bar the way.","Ben, the soccer-mad Englander, found her considering the wrong lock. \"Do you deplore me yet?\"","\"I cherish our friendship. I may eat my words about your hospitality.\"","\"Excellent. A loyal guest.\"","He led her back to the file labelled IN RE THE BLUE CABINET. Its peculiar numbering explained the toy train route. The solver had to go about reading the stops in order, not search every locked compartment. A child could likewise figure out the answer without knowing what the Latin heading meant. The phrase simply signalled the subject of a fictional file. It did not turn a game into a legal document.","A humid breeze entered from the conservatory. The high-quality paper had curled slightly, making one clue harder to read. Ben had been foresighted enough to keep flat copies. A fresh battery kept the toy signal working. He would not cut cost by making visitors decipher actual water damage. The museum might carry insurance, but he preferred dry cards. His taste in boss-board relations had improved when he stopped treating objections as personal betrayal. Mara suggested that this could serve as a clue in a different sort of game.","The actor’s grin was merciless; a frustrated Cassian could do nothing but wait. The group laughed, then an actor dropped a tin tray. The instantaneous crash made Cassian jerk toward the corridor. Mara saw his worry through the little window and waved before he could leave his role. It was a dropped object, not a falling person. He settled, but his relief warmed her more than victory would. He could be vulnerable to a startling sound without being transformed into a different man.","Rowan was trying to codify a rule whereby all rescue attempts earned a kiss. Ben said this would disorganize both the game and the banquet. Ethically, Rowan had no authority over the recipients. Conversely, he could volunteer his own cheek and see whether anyone cared. The suggestion received no bids. Mara thought the rejection would enhance his decision-making for at least three minutes.","She reached the cramped stair room, where a model supersonic aircraft hung above a table. Its label contained the missing number, and its shadow indicated the correct drawer. The multi-part clue was not unfeasible, merely harder when someone kept making her laugh. She had been forced to take account of a collaborator who wore an extravagant moustache and knew none of the answers. A collaborative game could test patience more thoroughly than a solitary one.","A film on the wall described the toy factory's old production regime. Its language was not part of the clue. Neither was the seminar poster about boss-board relations. Mara refused to delete useful information merely because a visitor might briefly confuse it with a game. In general, that would signify a guide note rather than a puzzle. The staff could enhance the distinction with a clear label. That would preclude the same argument recurring at each session.","\"You are better-behaved than Rowan,\" Ben told her.","\"A slender category.\"","\"Indeed.\"","On the matter of the next door, she had to devote a minute to listening. A recording gave three chimes, then a pause. The mid notes continued below a higher phrase. She intuitively followed the repeated sound instead of the loudest one. The marked handle released. The role gave him an intriguing freedom on and off stage. He could allude to tea as often as he wished. The actor behind it bowed and offered her a pearl made of painted wood.","The museum's activity bureau had asked Ben to recycle old materials. He had taken the request seriously: paper roses, wooden tokens and costumes from a closed show. The result could exemplify reuse without making every sign a lecture. A do-gooding impulse to tell guests how grateful they ought to be had been removed from the script. Their pleasure mattered enough to be allowed its own space.","Mara's next task was thorny. She could consolidate two tokens into a key, or use one to buy a false shortcut. The butler falsely advertised the second as instantaneous access. His broad wink made the trick obvious. She would not impinge on the story by demanding a real emergency exit. Common-sense curiosity would get her there.","Mara would not shatter a working object for a clue. The painted plaster might crumble, but the game expected a key. A small girl asked if the actors were accustomed to being teased. The teacher said yes, within the marked game. They were not unconcerned about a guest who pulled a sleeve or blocked a passage. The rule was in keeping with the rest: play freely, notice people. Mara could grow up without giving up the pleasure of pretending. She was not willfully ruining the butler's day by being quicker than he expected.","The final sign invited her to scale back on guesses and choose what the missing man actually wanted. Three portraits showed a crown, a sword and a teacup. She laughed, took the teacup token and opened the door."],"questions":[],"wordCount":950,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0203\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：失踪公爵与木头珍珠</div><h1>2. A Rescue Worth Repeating","volume":"婚后外传：失踪公爵与木头珍珠","anchor":"chapter-0204","paragraphs":["Cassian was waiting with a rose in his teeth. It was made of folded paper and tasted, he informed her, disappointingly of dust. Mara took it from him before allowing the promised kiss. He caught her close enough to make the butler clear his throat. His final duty was limited to the farewell speech. The actor wanted to finish his last line. Her husband looked faintly resentful of the interruption.","\"The kidnap is concluded,\" the butler announced. \"The ransom has been paid.\"","\"A peculiar business model,\" Mara said.","\"High-quality service,\" Cassian replied.","The collective sigh from the waiting guests suggested they had expected a longer kiss. Ben brought the witnesses into the room. The soccer-mad Englander declared them winners by a margin nobody understood. He was delighted enough to eat his words about ducal slowness. Their prize was a private museum visit followed by dinner. Mara said she would cherish it, provided the dinner did not involve finding her husband behind another wardrobe.","The file IN RE THE BLUE CABINET had one last page. It explained how guests could redeem their wooden pearl for a real keepsake at the gift desk. The small carved bird was generously made by a local apprentice. Its maker would receive the agreed payment for every one used. A gift could be charming without falsely suggesting that another person's unpaid labour had no cost.","Her quiet would always be vulnerable to a joke from Rowan. The humid afternoon had softened the paper roses. Cassian gathered a bundle while Mara helped the staff recycle the damaged ones. Ben would devote tomorrow to resetting the rooms. He would delete the confusing card, whereby the unnecessary detour could disappear. He could consolidate the useful feedback and codify the changes without writing an entirely new show. A foresighted designer expected an audience to find what his rehearsal team had missed. The first session had indeed given him plenty of ideas.","One guest had thought the model supersonic aircraft was a clue to run faster. It was not. Mara said that a common-sense sign could preclude that misunderstanding. Ben would figure out a clearer place for the number, then check it with someone who had not seen the game. The correction might exemplify good design more usefully than another page about how clever the designer had been.","At the banquet, Cassian sat beside her with the carved bird in his pocket. She was wearing his coat because the conservatory had cooled. His primal contentment was no secret to the table. Rowan deplored their smugness. \"Grow up,\" he said, while attempting to balance two desserts on one plate.","\"Likewise,\" Cassian replied.","The old poster about boss-board relations remained visible in the hall. Ben explained that the company's board had once rejected his dream of a far larger museum. The plan was unfeasible at the proposed price. He had accused them of lacking imagination, then found a smaller house that suited the games better. Good decision-making had been neither his sole gift nor the board's alone. He might still cut cost on unnecessary grandeur. He would not scale back on paying the actors or on repairs.","A volunteer asked whether the museum would carry insurance for the next season. Ben said yes, and also a spare kettle. Instantaneous tea service mattered to one particular duke. Mara lifted her cup in agreement. Conversely, a game that required everyone to remain unconcerned about a broken rail would not deserve a better-behaved audience. The organisers had fixed the actual problem before opening. The joke could remain a joke because somebody had done the work.","On the matter of next year's mystery, Rowan proposed a multi-day expedition with a captive prince. Mara considered it as long as it took Cassian to put his hand over hers. \"Perhaps one afternoon,\" she said.","\"A collaborative compromise,\" he replied.","Ben was not going to let a do-gooding urge disorganize another birthday into compulsory entertainment. He would ask what people wanted, then go about planning it. That seemed ethically straightforward and, to his relief, less exhausting. The party could redefine a successful fundraiser as an enjoyable gathering that actually raised money. Nobody had to endure a lecture to deserve cake.","Mara wandered into the hall after supper. The mid section of the painted backdrop showed a garden path beneath an enormous moon. The image reminded her of a place in her childhood that no longer existed. She touched the frame. Her affection for it was not a demand that Ben keep it forever. A loyal memory could survive a thing changing form.","Cassian joined her. \"Another clue?\"","\"A thought.\"","He waited without filling the pause. She liked that about him: his patience was not always natural, but he offered it generously. Her instincts had once interpreted stillness as an impending trap. Intuitively, she could now recognise this one as rest. The room was cramped, full of painted nonsense and entirely safe for the quiet they were sharing.","He asked whether he might steal the evening back from the party. \"Willfully?\" she asked.","\"Completely.\"","They slipped onto the terrace with the paper rose. A thorny real stem caught his sleeve; she freed it before it could impinge on his expensive cuff. He was laughing when she pulled him close. She wanted to redefine this rescue as a recurring arrangement. He agreed before learning the terms.","Behind the glass, Ben rang a bell to announce coffee. Nobody came. \"The evening is ours,\" Mara said, taking his hand. No one called them back unnecessarily. The retirement celebration continued in smaller conversations, on and off stage, while the missing duke and his rescuer took a very long time to return."],"questions":[],"wordCount":946,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0204\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：失踪公爵与木头珍珠</div><h1>3. Twenty-Seven Destinations","volume":"婚后外传：地图上的婚礼","anchor":"chapter-0205","paragraphs":["Celia's wedding had acquired twenty-seven destinations and no agreed honeymoon. Leon had chosen the resort. Celia had chosen a frontier village where she wanted to finish a photography project. Both believed the other had said yes. Mara discovered this while holding a delicate china swan and wearing a dress far too expensive to mediate an argument beside a luggage trolley.","\"OK,\" Cassian said, taking charge of the breakable bird. Cassian took the swan before she could break it by accident. \"Nice start,\" he murmured.","The wedding house stood on the outskirts of a county town, with a lawn overlooking grassland. A gardener had begun to dig a narrow bed for new roses. The terrain beyond sloped toward an old cemetery. A water tower was the familiar landmark at the edge of town. A holy statue watched the road from the chapel gate; Celia, who was mildly superstitious, had asked the florist not to cover its face. She was obsessed with light at this hour. She was winter-born and adored the clear air, though Leon had imagined a tropical paradise. They had a salient problem that flowers could not hide.","Celia's photographs filled the reception rooms. Boston appeared above a playground where she had taught a free class. Her work had reached the forefront of a small independent exhibition. A picture from Michigan showed a mother repairing a bicycle; Missouri supplied the broad prairie behind a different family. These were not interchangeable postcards. She would not reduce the people to scenery for her own story. Her earlier college-level course had taught technique, and the people themselves had taught her to look longer.","Leon had fallen in love with her at an exhibition in Vermont. Its New England winter was a world away from his usual haunt, a loud restaurant in Washington. His business empire made travel easy. Her work made staying in one locality important. He thought he was offering latitude when he said they could fly wherever she wished. She heard a promise that he would wait while she worked. Neither had checked what the words meant to the other.","A wedding trend had placed guests from downtown offices beside rural photographers who scarcely knew each other. On average, four guests shared a table. A wall map marked the Tropic of Cancer. Guests could take in the exhibition before sitting down. The interior was temporarily a gallery. In India, Celia had waited at the edge of a jungle with a local habitat guide. Below it, a photograph from India faced one from Greece. A sea horizon in Spain was labelled with its longitude and latitude. Celia had learned to orient a camera by sunlight before she could afford proper equipment. The pictures were fulfilling in their own right. Leon was proud of them, but his seating plan had made their locations the themes for dining tables. Peer pressure from his event planner had done the rest.","Mara would enlist Cassian to move the table marked Russia away from the draft. A family from Norway had been placed there and immediately asked whether anyone had confused the two countries. The Sweden table stood by the fireplace. The joke could be funny once; another one would be predictable. Celia looked primarily exhausted. Two instructions arrived simultaneously, and she looked ready for uncontrolled laughter. She needed to socialize with guests, not explain a map all evening.","Her itinerary included Andorra, where she had met a ceramic artist; a repaired bowl from that trip sat beside the china swan. It was worth seeing in its own right. England held a different memory. At Stratford-on-Avon she had photographed a retired actor outside his favourite pub. Britain was not simply another square on a wedding chart, and New England was not part of it. The planner had merged their decorative flags. Cassian fixed the mistake without announcing it to the room.","Near the entrance, two photographs had been taken in Rome and Vatican City on different days. A visiting priest recognised the holy procession in one and wanted to know about the people outside the frame. Celia answered eagerly. For a moment, the unfocused anxiety left her face. She could rediscover the trip without pretending it had all been paradise. She had lost her wallet in Rome and spent a cold evening furious with herself.","On the one hand, Leon wanted to take care of her. On the other, he had begun making arrangements in her name. He was not trying to invalidate her work. He was simply used to paying someone to remove an obstacle. Time, however, was scarce. She could not promise a photograph because an assistant had found a faster flight. Being in his orbit did not mean surrendering her own timetable.","Mara found Celia outside near the graveyard. A wind from the northwest lifted her veil. \"It feels like hell in there,\" she said, then looked guiltily at the holy statue. \"Sorry.\"","\"It probably has heard worse,\" Mara replied.","Celia's best-known project showed habitation beside an industrial zone, from a poor slum on the edge of a province to a new suburb built without a meeting place. Leon wanted to amass enough sponsorship to fund a book. She was grateful. She did not want a sponsor to choose which people looked sufficiently hopeful. His offer was thought-provoking, and not in the way he intended.","A culinary gift from Austin waited beside a parcel sent by a friend in California. Another friend had come from Canada, wearing the scarf Celia had left on a campus bench years before. The Commonwealth reference on an old invitation referred to a lecture, not the guest's nationality. Mara checked the names before sending anyone to a table labelled with a place they did not inhabit.","At dusk, Cassian found his wife on the lawn, studying the outward perfection of the house. \"Do you believe in wedding miracles?\" she asked.","\"Primarily in asking better questions before the cake is cut.\"","She leaned against him. Celia's camera was on the bench. The bride herself was walking toward the gate."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1010,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0205\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：地图上的婚礼</div><h1>1. The Bride at the Gate","volume":"婚后外传：地图上的婚礼","anchor":"chapter-0206","paragraphs":["Celia did not flee the wedding. She walked to the old cemetery because it was the quietest place in the locality. Leon followed her, then stopped when she lifted a hand. \"OK,\" Leon said. \"I’ll wait.\" Mara remained by the lawn gate with Cassian, close enough to help if asked and far enough away to leave them room. A winter-born bride had chosen clear air over a crowded reception room. That did not invalidate the marriage.","The holy statue overlooked the grassland. Celia had often photographed places where habitation pressed against a boundary: a frontier settlement, a slum beside expensive housing, a suburb without a playground. Those pictures were primarily about people making homes. Leon had admired them as bold compositions. She usually spent, on average, two weeks in one place. She wanted him to understand the waiting, repeated visits and ordinary meals behind each frame.","\"I thought I was giving you paradise,\" he said.","\"You were giving me an itinerary.\"","The remark was not cruel. Its salient accuracy made him sit beside her on the low wall. \"I’ve made a hell of a mess,\" Leon admitted. He had planned a resort because he was tired. She had promised to return to a county village because someone there was trusting her with a story. The quiet zone outside the gate put their actual wishes at the forefront. They could not merge those two needs by calling every disagreement unfocused anxiety. On the one hand, they were beginning a marriage. On the other, their lives had not stopped being real.","Mara fetched the camera from the house. The first image Celia showed Leon was from Boston, after a playground class had finished. The next came from Michigan, taken a year after the bicycle picture. Missouri supplied a family album loaned by the people she had photographed on the prairie. His wealth could not shorten that relationship into a weekend. \"You cannot reduce a friendship to a travel slot,\" she said.","He needed a minute to take in what she meant. He listened. In Vermont, he had first met the woman who could wait forty minutes for somebody to stop performing for her lens. In New England, he had learnt to enjoy a snowy afternoon without his telephone. Their old haunt in Washington had once seemed glamorous; now it chiefly represented how loudly he could arrange things. His business empire did not have to invade every conversation. He laughed sadly at the thought.","They went through the map one place at a time. Andorra was where she had learned to photograph pottery without turning it into a pretty blur. Spain had taught her to ask before placing a working fisherman in a composition. The England folder held the Stratford-on-Avon actor, who had insisted that she stay for tea. They spoke simultaneously, then stopped laughing to let each other finish. He had been in his prime when the theatre closed and had found a new audience at a local school. His smile was not a symbol of effortless resilience. It belonged to him.","Inside, Cassian helped the planner separate Rome from Vatican City on the programme. The actual mistake was minor; the assumption that nobody would care was less nice. A tray of china cups threatened to tip, and he caught it. A guest from Sweden helped fold the corrected cards while a woman from Norway carried tea. The family who had objected at the Russia table now laughed over a picture of themselves looking intensely suspicious. Their humour came after someone had listened, not before.","Leon asked whether Celia wanted to cancel the resort. She said yes to the first reservation, not to every future holiday. They were short of time for the project. A later week together in the countryside would be heaven. She was not asking him to dig a trench between their lives. She wanted latitude to finish the commitment she had made before the wedding. Time remained scarce, and he could not orbit every demand from his office. Peer pressure to host a perfect wedding had not helped. The request was far more specific than the quarrel had sounded.","The couple walked past the graveyard toward the house. Celia explained the Tropic of Cancer on her map, then the latitude of a later photograph from Greece. The precise longitude was in her field notes. She did not carry every number in her head, and nobody expected her to. A college-level course could teach coordinates; it could not decide what was worth looking at. The trend toward instant travel stories made that patience harder to explain. He had been too busy to socialize with the people she knew. Her work was thought-provoking in its own right, even when it did not offer a clear answer.","Mara saw Leon pause at a picture from India. The jungle habitat in its background had nearly swallowed the small path she followed. He asked whether she had been frightened. She said yes. She had been superstitious about a broken camera strap for a week afterward, then had replaced it. No magical sign had caused the trouble. A worn strap had. The practical answer was less fabulous than the story she had told herself.","On the outskirts, the wind had shifted northwest. The unfamiliar terrain no longer looked threatening. An old provincial landmark, the water tower, helped orient the arriving guests. The province had restored it instead of demolishing it. Celia had once climbed to its interior to photograph the town. Leon had thought the image was a city abroad. Now he asked her to show him the people she knew here.","Cassian took Mara's hand. \"How are we doing?\"","\"The bride has returned. We have not started a war between tables.\"","\"A nice outcome.\"","\"Don't tempt fate. A Place for the Photograph","volume":"婚后外传：地图上的婚礼","anchor":"chapter-0207","paragraphs":["\"OK,\" Celia said when the florist asked if she was ready. The ceremony began twenty minutes late. Celia walked across the lawn without pretending the delay had been a clever bridal entrance. Leon met her at the chapel door, then stood beside her while the music finished. Their winter-born photographer friend took one picture and put the camera down. Some moments could be attended before they were collected.","Mara had taken peer pressure out of the seating plan by removing the country labels. They were now captions beside Celia's photographs, where they belonged. Andorra accompanied the pottery artist's portrait, with the artist's own words. Boston stood beneath the children tidying their playground. Michigan and Missouri had separate stories, not a promise that every part of America looked alike. The prairie photograph faced a window overlooking real grassland outside.","Leon had asked Celia to help him write one sentence for the book proposal. It would not reduce a life to a landscape. The sentence was still imperfect; it no longer assumed that sponsorship bought a simpler story. The quiet countryside would be heaven after the busy reception interior. The project might amass support slowly. They had latitude to try. His empire could remain financially useful without becoming the bride's entire orbit.","Leon laughed at the abandoned chart. \"What the hell was I thinking?\" His acceptance of the mistake was the salient change. At the reception, a guest from Vermont told Cassian about the first exhibition. Another from Washington described Celia persuading a noisy room to listen. Mara heard the stories from New England merge into a new memory at the table. A friend from California asked Leon whether he would visit again. He said he would ask his wife when she was free. The friend from Canada photographed their embrace. The parcel from Austin, Texas, supplied a spicy snack that made another guest reach for water. The change was small enough to sound unremarkable. It made Celia smile.","Her old campus friend had found the Commonwealth lecture invitation tucked in a book. It was returned with affection, while visitors from Britain read the actor’s message. A postcard from England lay beside the wedding book. The retired actor at Stratford-on-Avon had sent a message about making room for the other player's line. His signature was much larger than the message. A pair of guests from Norway and Sweden argued fondly about whose folding of the programmes had been tidier. The woman who had disliked the Russia table toasted the new arrangement with a grin. Nice manners did not require pretending the first idea had been perfect.","Celia's map still marked Rome and Vatican City. Their different labels had prompted a conversation about where a picture ended and a story began. The holy procession was not more interesting than the people eating afterward. A guest recognised herself at the edge of the Rome photograph and asked for a copy. Celia promised one. The holy statue outside had received a stray ribbon anyway; a child had tied it around the base, carefully below the figure.","Mara had been unpopular with the planner when she changed the tables. She preferred that to an evening of unhappy jokes. The county town had once been Celia's favourite haunt. Celia could rediscover a downtown street as readily as a foreign capital. She had wanted to escape its outskirts, then returned to photograph them. Its old cemetery was not merely a gloomy landmark. She knew the caretaker, whose son had made the benches. Its quiet province was full of habitation that never appeared in a tourist picture. A slum on one edge and a prosperous suburb on the other both contained people who disliked being reduced to a trend.","Leon would not join the first part of her frontier project. He had commitments of his own. They would meet afterward in a small resort with no sponsored activities. Celia called the compromise paradise, then laughed at herself. She did not want to turn a week off into another standard they could fail. It was primarily a chance to socialize without a camera between them. Time would still be scarce. They could choose how to use what they had.","Mara stepped outside with Cassian after the cake. A breeze from the northwest cooled her shoulders. The terrain beside the lawn caught the last light; beyond it lay the cemetery wall. \"Not a bad place to talk,\" she said.","\"The locality offers surprisingly good marriage advice.\"","\"Mostly in the form of a wall to sit on.\"","He drew her nearer. \"I shall put it at the forefront of my recommendations.\"","The pair had spent years learning to orient themselves toward one another without abandoning every other direction. On the one hand, she liked being found. On the other, she enjoyed finding him first. She suspected this balance would remain thought-provoking well beyond their current capacity for explaining it. He was not a landmark that stayed still while she travelled. He chose the path beside her.","Celia joined them carrying a china plate, with a delicate slice of cake balanced on it. \"I enlisted you to admire flowers,\" she said. \"This was rather more work.\"","\"A fulfilling deviation,\" Mara replied.","The children had their own play zone at the far end of the room. A small guest ran past with a toy satellite, sending it into an uncontrolled orbit around his own head. Cassian caught the loose string before it struck the plate. Celia laughed. The Tropic of Cancer on her abandoned map could wait until morning; so could the notes from Spain and the next request for an address. Wherever she worked, there would be another precise thing to record. Tonight, the main thing was here.","Mara was not obsessed with keeping a perfect record. She did, however, ask for one photograph beneath the holy statue, before the light vanished. Cassian whispered something that made her throw her head back laughing. The picture was unfocused at one edge and entirely unlike the approved wedding style. It became her favourite.","Inside, the music rose. The superstitious bride forgot her fear that an argument meant bad luck. The groom forgot his predictable impulse to solve everything at once. They danced, and the room found enough latitude for joy."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1045,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0207\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：地图上的婚礼</div><h1>3.
